Maybe the function is supposed to support that but doesn't yet.\n\nLet's examine test_export.py more carefully. It imports `export_csv` from export, `render_rows` from render, `column_totals` from totals. It defines several tests.\n\ntest_export_without_totals: assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]]) == \"1,2\\n3,4\"\n\ntest_export_appends_totals: assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n\nSo export_csv is expected to accept a `totals` keyword argument. But the current export.py only defines `def export_csv(rows):`. So it doesn't handle the `totals` parameter. That's likely the bug: export is missing totals row because the function doesn't check for a totals flag and append a totals row.\n\nAlso, render_rows function in render.py currently doesn't have a `mark_last` parameter, but there is a test test_render_marks_last_row_as_total that expects render_rows to have a `mark_last` parameter. However, the current render_rows definition is `def render_rows(rows): return [\",\".join(str(v) for v in r) for r in rows]`. It doesn't support marking last row.\n\nBut the test_export_appends_totals expects export_csv to append a totals row when `totals=True`. How is that implemented? Maybe export_csv should compute totals using column_totals and then append a row marked as total. Let's see if export.py needs to be modified to handle the `totals` argument.\n\nLet's check if export.py has been updated elsewhere? The file content we read earlier was:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\n\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nThat's it. No handling of totals.\n\nSo we need to modify export.py to optionally append a totals row when a `totals` flag is True. Also, we might need to modify render_rows to support marking the last row as \"TOTAL\" or something. But the test_export_appends_totals expects the output: \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". The totals row seems to have \"TOTAL\" in the first column, and the actual totals in the other columns. The column_totals function returns [sum(r[i] for r in rows) for i in range(len(rows[0]))]. For rows [[1,2],[3,4]], column_totals would be [1+3, 2+4] = [4,6]. But the expected totals row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". So the first column is replaced with the string \"TOTAL\", and the rest are the summed values.\n\nLooking at test_render_marks_last_row_as_total: it tests render_rows with mark_last=True and expects [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"] for rows [[1,2],[3,4]]. So the test command is `uv run --offline pytest -q`. We need to run that after making changes.\n\nBut first, let's edit export.py to add totals functionality.\n\nWe need to import column_totals from totals.py. Then modify export_csv to accept an optional `totals` parameter (default False). If totals is True, compute the totals row and append it.\n\nThe totals row should be formatted as a CSV row. According to the expected output \"TOTAL,4,6\", the first element is the string \"TOTAL\", then the total values for each column. But if the data rows have a first column that is not a number (like an identifier), we might need to handle it differently. However, based on the simple test case with rows [[1,2],[3,4]], the totals row is [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6]. So we can construct the totals row as [\"TOTAL\"] + list(totals). But totals is computed by column_totals(rows), which returns [4,6]. So [\"TOTAL\"] + [4,6] gives [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6]. That matches.\n\nBut what if the rows have more than two columns? Then the totals row would have \"TOTAL\" plus all column totals. That seems reasonable.\n\nSo the plan:\n- Edit export.py:\n  - Add import `from totals import column_totals`\n  - Modify function `export_csv(rows, totals=False)` to accept `totals` parameter.\n  - If `totals` is True, compute `totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + list(column_totals(rows))` and then append this row to the rendered rows.\n  - Then return the joined string.\n\nBut we need to be careful: column_totals expects rows to be numeric or convertible to numbers. In the test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells, it returns [\"\", 3] for mixed text and numbers. That suggests column_totals handles non-numeric by returning empty string for that column? Actually the function is `sum(r[i] for r in rows)`. If r[i] is a string, sum will raise TypeError. But the test expects [\"\", 3]. Maybe the function is different in actual code? But the test expects it to return [\"\", 3] for [[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]. How is that possible? Maybe the rows are actually numbers except for the first column which is text, and the sum ignores text? No, sum would fail.\n\nPerhaps the test is for a different version of totals.py. Or maybe the function is meant to be used only on numeric columns after the first? Not sure.\n\nGiven the immediate issue is missing totals row in export, and the test_export_appends_totals expects a specific output, we should follow the test expectation. The test expects the totals row to be \"TOTAL,4,6\" for input [[1,2],[3,4]]. So we can implement exactly that: if totals=True, append a row where the first column is \"TOTAL\" and the rest are the sum of each column.\n\nWe can compute the sums manually without using column_totals if needed, but we have the function. Let's see if column_totals works with the test data. If we call column_totals([[1,2],[3,4]]), it should return [4,6]. Let's test mentally: sum(r[0] for r in rows) = 1+3 = 4, sum(r[1] for r in rows) = 2+4 = 6. So [4,6]. Good.\n\nSo we can use column_totals.\n\nNow, edit export.py. We need to make sure we match the exact oldText for the edit. The current content of export.py is:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\n\n\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nWe want to change it to:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    lines = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n        # Compute totals row\n        totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + list(column_totals(rows))\n        lines.append(\",\".join(str(v) for v in totals_row))\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\nBut we need to be careful with the formatting and indentation.\n\nAlternatively, we could keep the function signature as `export_csv(rows, totals=False)` or `export_csv(rows, totals=False):`. The test calls `export_csv(..., totals=True)`, so it expects the parameter.\n\nWe also need to consider that render_rows returns a list of strings (each row already formatted as CSV). Currently, render_rows(rows) returns [ \"1,2\", \"3,4\" ] for the example. If we append a totals row, we need to format it as a CSV string as well. The totals_row list contains [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6], so we should join with commas: \",\".join(str(v) for v in totals_row). That would produce \"TOTAL,4,6\". Good.\n\nSo the new code would be:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    lines = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n        totals_values = column_totals(rows)\n        totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + list(totals_values)\n        lines.append(\",\".join(str(v) for v in totals_row))\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\nBut we need to check if column_totals returns a list of numbers or mixed types.
